hearing-3-questions.md

Expert hearing 3 — questions from plenary round 1

med (Sports medicine)

  1. Sports cardiology: for runners ≥65 (and ≥35 previously sedentary), should a first training run >3 h or a first marathon/ultra require a clinical check, or is self-attestation after PAR-Q+ acceptable? Race cardiac-arrest incidence by age and distance.
  2. Women's health: return to running after childbirth (Goom/Donnelly/Brockwell 2019): starting dose and progression from ~12 weeks; running during pregnancy: volume/intensity guidance by trimester; stop signs.
  3. Recurring pain: when should repeated non-consecutive pain episodes (e.g. 3 in 6 weeks) trigger referral?

sys (Knowledge modelling & rule engineering)

  1. Validator independence: if the validator shares demand formulas with the generator, how independent is it? Patterns: N-version / independent re-implementation, property-based testing, runtime assertion checking.
  2. Rule expression grammar and machine records: recommended formats for a deterministic rule base on a website (e.g. JSON Logic, DMN FEEL, CEL, custom DSL) — trade-offs for explainability, testability and handling of unknown/missing values (three-valued logic).
hearing-3-sys-acad.md

Hearing 3 — Knowledge modelling & rule engineering (sys) — academic & official base

Access limits: 8 WebSearch queries (shared budget). I opened no primary sources, so no claim is VERIFIED. (prior knowledge) marks material I did not re-check this session. What this base cannot see: how production rule-engine teams (or Runna/TrainAsONE-type products) actually split generator and validator; real bug rates per grammar; developer-experience data. There is no sport-specific literature here; everything below comes from software engineering.

Q1 — How independent is a validator that shares demand formulas with the generator?

  • Shared code means correlated failures. Knight & Leveson (1986) had 27 versions written independently from one specification and ran 1,000,000 tests on them. Coincident failures were far above what statistical independence predicts, because programmers made similar mistakes on the same hard inputs. Their conclusion: reliability analysis must assume dependent errors. Sources: UVA record; NASA NTRS; Leveson in RISKS 1:37; reply to critics. Consensus; CORROBORATED.
  • Implication (reasoning, not evidence): this was separate code from one spec, and errors were still correlated. A validator that calls the same formula as the generator cannot detect any error inside that formula. Its independence for that formula is zero. It can still catch errors elsewhere, such as in assembly, ordering or caps.
  • Oracle theory. Barr, Harman, McMinn, Shahbaz & Yoo (IEEE TSE 2015) classify test oracles as specified (from a spec or contracts), derived (from the implementation or its documents), implicit (crashes) or human. Where no full spec exists, partial oracles from metamorphic relations are the recommended route (UCL; course mirror). A 2026 review grades pseudo-oracles (alternative implementations) by "reference independence" (arXiv 2607.05031). Majority view; CREDIBLE.
  • Three patterns:
  • N-version / independent re-implementation. Diversity helps but is not independence (above). Diversity is greater when the second version comes from a different representation, e.g. a table instead of code, or a different author (prior knowledge). Consensus; CORROBORATED.
  • Property-based and metamorphic testing. QuickCheck (Claessen & Hughes 2000) checks properties over random inputs. Metamorphic testing checks relations between outputs, e.g. "more weeks available ⇒ no higher peak ramp", so it needs no second formula. In a controlled experiment it found more faults than assertion checking (Zhang, Chan, Tse et al., J. Software 2009, HKU; TR). Metamorphic testing can be run with property-based tools (Alzahrani et al., ENASE 2022). Emerging/majority; CREDIBLE (one controlled experiment).
  • Runtime assertion checking (design by contract). Postconditions are evaluated on every output, e.g. "weekly km ≤ cap" or "no two hard days adjacent". Overhead is the main obstacle in heavy numeric code (Dahlgren & Devanbu). It was small or insignificant in an Eiffel study (arXiv 1208.3337). Static analysis can remove checks (PPDP 2016). Majority view; CORROBORATED. Reasoning: for a plan of ≤200 weeks, the cost is negligible. Contracts only catch what they state, so their independence depends on writing them as limits, not as copies of the formulas.

Q2 — Rule expression grammar and machine records

OptionExplainabilityTestability / analysisUnknown / missing values
DMN decision tables + FEEL (OMG standard)Tables are readable by domain expertsFormal algorithms detect overlapping and missing rules (sweep-line), implemented in an open-source editor (Calvanese et al., BPM 2016, arXiv 1603.07466; IS 2018 vol 78:112–125). CORROBORATEDFEEL is three-valued (true/false/null). Null currently mixes "missing" and "type error" (false and "a" → null). OMG issue DMN16-91 proposes B-FEEL with null for missing data only (DMN16-91; DMN13-106; Trisotech; CEUR 3229). CORROBORATED
CEL (Google)Expressions only; no tablesNot Turing-complete; designed for speed and safety&&/` are commutative. Error and unknown are distinct values. false && unknown` = false, which is Kleene-like and matches SQL. Supports partial evaluation (cel-go; README). CREDIBLE (same origin, vendor docs)
JsonLogicRules are plain JSON, easy to store and versionSmall operator set; ports in JS, PHP, Haskell, Rust and .NETTwo-valued truthiness: null → falsy, and empty array falsy (JS/PHP differ, spec breaks the tie). missing/missing_some list absent keys; var takes defaults (json-logic-js ref; json-everything). CREDIBLE (secondary docs; spec page not retrieved)
Custom DSLWhatever is designedNo external tooling; the team must build its own analysisWhatever is designed (prior knowledge)
  • Three-valued logic variants. Kleene strong logic gives F∧U = U∧F = F. McCarthy left-sequential logic gives F∧U = F but U∧F = U, so operand order changes the result (arXiv 2403.14576). CREDIBLE. Whether a missing input silently becomes "false" (JsonLogic truthiness), "unknown" (CEL, FEEL) or an error is a grammar choice, and it changes the outputs.

Overall:

  • Shared formulas give no independence for those formulas (consensus).
  • Metamorphic properties and contract limits are the documented ways to get partial independence without a second implementation.
  • Of the four grammars, only DMN has published completeness and overlap analysis.
  • None of the four has been evaluated in a training-plan context. UNKNOWN.
hearing-3-sys-field.md

Hearing 3 — Knowledge modelling & rule engineering (sys) — FIELD base

Process limits: 8 WebSearch queries, the full shared budget. No primary page was opened, so nothing here is VERIFIED. The base is industry practice and engine documentation.

Q1. How independent is a validator that shares demand formulas with the generator?

What the field reports

  • Shared specification means correlated faults. In Knight & Leveson (1986), 27 versions were built independently from one specification and run against 1M tests. Their failures were not independent: different programmers made the same logical errors, and some distinct errors failed on the same inputs. A 2026 replication (48 agent-written versions) again found substantial common-mode failure on ambiguous spec parts. Defects cluster at unspecified boundary conditions (1990 follow-up, second-hand). Grade: CORROBORATED. Strength: consensus.
  • Shared code is worse than a shared spec. Inference: comparing variants of one code base cannot detect a bug common to all of them (arXiv 1810.02718). A validator importing the generator's demand function cannot catch errors in that formula. Grade: CREDIBLE (reasoned).
  • Field patterns, ordered from least to most independent:
  1. Shared formula plus runtime assertions (design-by-contract invariants checked before/after each call; Eiffel docs, Leiden thesis). Catch impossible states, not a wrong formula; in production a failed check moves to a safe state (contested whether asserts ship). Grade: CORROBORATED.
  2. Property-based testing checks invariants over generated inputs rather than examples, for instance "credit never increases amount due" or "discount ≤ price". Castro (J. Intell. Inf. Syst., DOI 10.1007/s10844-014-0335-2) applies QuickCheck to industrial business rules. Grade: CORROBORATED. Properties stated without calling the formula ("weekly load ≤ cap", "no hard session within 48 h of another") are independent of it.
  3. Metamorphic relations compare related runs, so no exact oracle is needed. Example: if available days rise and all else is equal, total volume should not fall. Effectiveness depends entirely on the relations chosen (HKU, Chen/Tse papers; Nottingham review). Grade: CORROBORATED.
  4. Independent re-implementation (N-version) gives the most independence, yet it is still limited by a shared spec. Grade: CORROBORATED.
  • Quantified independence for any of these patterns in a rule-based plan generator: UNKNOWN.

Q2. Rule-expression grammar, machine records, unknown/missing values

FormatExplainability / testability (field view)Missing / unknown handlingGrade
JSON LogicPlain JSON: easy to store, diff, ship to browser; many ports.No third truth value. JS-style truthiness, except that [] is falsy, so null, 0 and "" are all falsy and a missing 0 looks the same as "no". missing and missing_some list absent keys. Whether a var default applies to an explicit null differs between implementations: a 2025 json-logic-java PR changed it to "absent only".CORROBORATED (deepwiki API reference, GitHub PR); semantics drift is ALLEGED (single PR)
DMN / FEELOMG standard. Decision tables are readable by non-programmers. Engines: FEEL-Scala, feelin (JS); extensions not portable.Three-valued (true/false/null), but null means both "missing" and "evaluation error". DMN 1.6 adds B-FEEL, which turns null-from-error into two-valued logic. Camunda advises explicit guards (x != null and …, get or else(), assert()); guard order matters.CORROBORATED (Camunda docs, Trisotech, OMG issues)
CEL (Google)Not Turing-complete, linear-time and free of mutation, so it is safe to evaluate. It has a static type-check phase and has() for testing whether a field is present.Distinguishes error from unknown. && and ` are commutative and absorb: false && <error> gives false and true<unknown> gives true`. Otherwise the result is the error or unknown, together with the attributes that would decide it, which supports partial evaluation.CORROBORATED (cel-go README, cel-spec; mirrors of one origin)
Custom DSLFull control over explanations; you own parser, tests, docs.Whatever you specify.No field comparison found: UNKNOWN
  • No head-to-head comparison of JSON Logic, FEEL and CEL turned up: UNKNOWN.
  • A pattern that recurs across all three: make presence explicit (missing, has(), != null guards) and do not rely on implicit null semantics. Grade: CORROBORATED (three ecosystems' docs and forums).

What this base cannot see

How any running app encodes or QA-tests its rules; DSL maintenance cost; failure statistics by format.

Opening positions

opening-contextrealist.md

Opening — Context Realist (contextrealist)

Our evidence is mostly from adult recreational cohorts, about 80% male (hearing-1). The method must also hold for a 16-year-old, a 68-year-old, a night-shift nurse, 3 h/week, or 100 km in 4 weeks.

1. What the method must get right

  1. Two level axes (crit 2, 4). One axis is performance: WMA age-grade % (WG3, CORROBORATED). The other is tissue tolerance: training age, recent km, injury in the last 12 months, other-sport background. Prior injury is the strongest risk factor (hearing-1, CORROBORATED); ex-cyclists and swimmers are high-risk (WG3, CREDIBLE). Volume follows tissue tolerance, pace follows performance (design choice).
  2. Hard gates before generation (crit 7): PAR-Q+ 2023; age ≥65 + high intensity → clinical assessment (ESC 2020); under 18 → UKA distance tables, lower table by default; pregnancy → maintenance only for prior runners, ACOG stop signs; postpartum <12 weeks → no plan (Goom 2019). All WG4, CORROBORATED (minors: federation norm).
  3. Time budget changes the method, not just the volume (crit 3, 9). No data exist below 3 h/week (hearing-1, UNKNOWN); frequency drives response (Montero & Lundby, CORROBORATED; cycling). With the daniels cap, a small budget means a short long run, so a marathon or ultra must fail feasibility openly and offer options, not quietly break the cap as Higdon Novice does (WG2, CORROBORATED).
  4. Horizon changes phase logic (crit 3). In 4 weeks or less there is no build, because the taper alone takes 2–3 weeks (WG1/WG2, CORROBORATED). It becomes maintain + taper plus a goal adjustment. Multi-year plans and improvement rates are UNKNOWN (hearing-1, factdesk1), so re-test and re-plan each season; never project gains.
  5. Sex without folklore (crit 8). Cycle-phase effects on performance are trivial (McNulty, CORROBORATED), so by default the plan is not periodised by cycle phase. Menstrual disruption triggers a REDs referral (IOC 2023, CORROBORATED). Men need a symptom-based screen too (LEAF-Q is women-only, WG5).

2. Biggest risk

Rules from the median recreational runner applied silently outside that population. Examples: the spike cap on ultra back-to-backs; age-formula HR zones (±10–12 bpm, WG1, CORROBORATED) for masters; elite detraining figures for beginners (WG3). Guard: every rule carries an evidence envelope of population tags. A profile outside it gets the conservative fallback and an explanation.

3. Proposal: a context-modifier table plus an edge-persona test grid

context_modifier { id, trigger, parameter, operation, value, grade, envelope, user_message }. Order: gates → caps → defaults; on conflict the most conservative value wins. Seed rows:

  • Novice, other-sport background, or injury in the last 12 months → deload_every = 3 (ALLEGED convention, WG1) and the slower progression profile (WG4, CORROBORATED).
  • Masters ≥50 → deload_every = 3 (ALLEGED), strength module on (CREDIBLE), HR zones from measured values only.
  • Night shift the day before → the key session becomes easy. This is an ALLEGED design default: there is no shift-work evidence, though sleep loss is CORROBORATED.
  • Fewer than 3 sessions a week → protect frequency before session length (CORROBORATED direction).

Test grid (crit 11, 13): time budgets (3/6/9/12 h) × horizons (4 wk/16 wk/2 yr) × the 5 distances × six profiles: novice, masters 65+, age 16, postpartum, prior bone stress injury, shift worker. Assert: no gate bypassed, no cap broken, every infeasible case yields a goal-adjustment message.

4. Requests

  1. Fact desk: does Frandsen 2025 report spike risk by sex, age or experience?
  2. Coach experts: what minimum weekly volume or long run do coaches accept for a finish-only marathon or 50–100 km on 3–4 h/week (labelled feasibility floor)?
opening-contrarian.md

Opening — Contrarian

1. Requirements

  1. No catch-up (C6, C7). A re-plan never puts missed load into the remaining weeks. Catching up creates the single-session spike linked to injury: HRR 1.64 for runs more than 10% longer than the 30-day longest run, and 2.28 above 100% (wg4 §1, CORROBORATED, observational). Missed work is dropped, and feasibility (C9) is checked again.
  2. No plan for infeasible requests (C3, C9). A 4-week first marathon or a 100 km on 3 h/week gets "change the goal or date", never a squeezed plan. The thresholds are ALLEGED or UNKNOWN (wg3 §4, factdesk1 §7), so they are conservative design defaults. The gate itself stays.
  3. Red flags block until the user clears them (C7). A cardiac or suspected-BSI flag stops the plan until the user attests to clearance. The attestation can't be given on the day the flag is raised. The pain model (default ≤3/10; the thresholds are CONTRADICTED, factdesk1 §2) never covers suspected BSI.
  4. Self-reports are unverified (C2). Users will overstate their longest run and PB to unlock harder plans. Caps use logged runs; unlogged claims get the slower profile for 2–4 weeks (design default; self-report accuracy UNKNOWN).
  5. No safety claims for adaptation (C8, C12). No study shows that adaptive plans reduce injury (factdesk1 §5).

2. Biggest failure mode: rule collisions

Rules that are sound alone can combine into nonsense:

  • Break. After a break, the 30-day longest run is 0. The cap then either blocks everything or falls back to claimed history.
  • Back-to-backs. Back-to-back long runs each pass a per-session cap. They are ALLEGED (wg1, wg2), and spike risk for ultra runners is UNKNOWN (wg4).
  • Short plans. In a 4-week plan, a taper plus a deload leaves no training at all.
  • Goal-shopping. Users can keep editing the date until the feasibility check passes.
  • Drop-out. C25K completion is 27% (wg3 §5) and novice attrition is 42–53% (hearing-1, CORROBORATED). A plan that only ever tightens loses people.

Guard: check the whole plan after every generation and adaptation.

3. Proposal: a plan validator plus an adversarial test suite

A separate module checks every plan output. It rejects or clamps any breach and logs the reason.

  • I1 Session cap. Warn above 1.10× the longest logged run of the last 30 days and block above 2.0×. If no run is logged in the window, use the return-to-run ladder, never claimed history.
  • I2 Two-day cap. Any 2 consecutive days total at most 1.3× the 30-day longest run (design default, ALLEGED).
  • I3 No catch-up. After an adaptation, no future week's load exceeds what the plan held for that week before.
  • I4 Red flags. An active red flag means zero running sessions.
  • I5 Minors. UKA maximum-distance table (wg4 §6).
  • I6 Base/build. Base and build together last at least 1 week; otherwise output a goal change.
  • I7 Goal edits. Every goal or date edit re-runs feasibility, and repeated failures are logged.
  • I8 Fuelling. Race fuelling never exceeds the intake tolerated in training (wg5).

The test suite (C11, C13) must pass before release:

  • about 30 edge personas (zero history, 6 weeks off, a 17-year-old marathoner, 8 weeks postpartum, a 100 km on 3 h/week, a 2-week plan, an inflated PB);
  • random input fuzzing.

4. Requests

  1. Frandsen 2025. How were runs after a gap of 30 days or more classified? The answer sets the I1 fallback.
  2. Regulation. Does a screening and red-flag engine fall under the EU MDR or FDA device rules (ALLEGED, wg4)? The answer decides the wording of "stop".
opening-executor.md

Opening — Executor

1. Requirements I will hold the architect to

  1. Fixed layers, run in order (C5, C11). Safety gate → feasibility → generation → adaptation → validation. Each rule gets its own priority class: in software practice, rules with equal priority run in arbitrary order (hearing-1 §5, COR).
  2. No literal numbers; a parameter registry instead (C5, C8). Each entry is {id, default, unit, grade, sources, school}. Many defaults rest on no evidence: deload frequency (wg1 §5, ALLEGED/UNK), return-after-break cuts (factdesk1 §4, UNK), goal-stretch caps (factdesk1 §6, UNK), pain ≤3/10 (factdesk1 §2, design default). Updating one must be a data edit, not a code change.
  3. Minimal load model (C6, C7). Distance/time, session RPE and time in zone (wg1 §3, COR). No ACWR, Banister or monotony gate (wg1 §3, CONTRADICTED/ALLEGED).
  4. Build in order of evidence strength (C1, C3). First build what is CORROBORATED: screening, red flags, the per-run spike cap, taper and race-anchored paces (wg4 §1, §3, §4; wg1 §5; factdesk1 §3). Then 50–100 km as an extension. Its determinants are associations only, back-to-backs are ALLEGED, and the road-ultra injury rate is UNK (wg1 §1, wg2 §4, wg4 §2).
  5. Testable (C13). Every rule needs a test where it fires and one where it does not.

2. Biggest risk

Risk: rules multiply and conflict silently (taper vs deload, ultra volume vs spike cap). An evidence update then breaks distant plans, and unsafe output reaches self-coached users.

Guard: treat the generator as untrusted. Re-check every plan and re-plan with an independent validator, and log each fired rule with its grade (C12).

3. Proposal: v0 rule set, validator and build order

Generator v0, about 15 rules:

  • PAR-Q+/ACSM screen, red-flag stop, minors' cap, pregnancy/postpartum gate (wg4)
  • Level from age-grade % plus recent km (wg3 §1)
  • Feasibility with a ±6–8% band and a marathon penalty (wg1 §7)
  • Phase split by weeks available
  • Intensity distribution (TID) preset, pyramidal by default; long-run preset daniels (wg1 §2, wg2 §1)
  • Deload every 4 weeks (labelled ALLEGED)
  • Taper: 3 weeks for the marathon, 2 weeks for shorter races; −40–60% volume; frequency kept
  • Adaptation: missed session, illness (Elliott gate), pain, re-anchor on a new result

Validator invariants:

  • I1: a run longer than 1.10× the longest run of the last 30 days → warning; above 2.0× → block (wg4 §1)
  • I2: no vigorous session while screening is unresolved
  • I3: the taper keeps session frequency
  • I4: no zones from age-predicted HRmax (wg1 §4)
  • I5: weekly totals equal the sum of their sessions
  • I6: every parameter used resolves to a graded registry entry
  • I7: flag (do not block) plans outside the volume and long-run bands of Knopp's plan corpus (wg2 §2)

Tests:

  • 5 golden personas with snapshot plans
  • Property tests on random profiles and durations of 2–200 weeks, asserting I1–I6
  • Replay tests for each adaptation event

Build order: registry and data model → safety layer and validator → 5K–marathon generation → adaptation → explanations → ultra.

4. Requests

  1. Legal: under the EU MDR or the FDA, does a "stop and see a professional" engine make the tool a medical device (wg4, ALLEGED)?
  2. Fact desk: how did Frandsen 2025 handle runners with sparse or no 30-day history? Design-choice default until answered: use the self-reported longest recent run, capped at the starting long run set by screening.
opening-expansionist.md

Opening — Expansionist (expansionist)

1. What the method must get right

  1. Predictions that improve with every data point (C9, C6). One race plus training data cut Vickers' marathon error from 381 (Riegel) to 228, and two races to 208 (wg1 §7, CORROBORATED). The feasibility engine must accept 1..n results plus weekly km and longest run. Its error band should tighten as inputs accumulate.
  2. Open the door to the widest population (C2, C3, C10). Run-walk must be a first-class session type, not a beginner workaround. Hottenrott 2016 found no difference in finish time (wg2-draft, CORROBORATED). Low-frequency (3-run) plans should be an explicit preset, labelled with ALLEGED outcomes (wg3 §3). C25K completion is 27.3% (wg3 §5, CORROBORATED): reach is won or lost here.
  3. Schools as presets, not forks (C8, C10). One engine should express Daniels, Hansons, Pfitzinger/classic, Galloway and Koop-ultra through named parameters: long-run cap, quality count, TID and taper length (wg2 implications 1, 3, 5–7; CORROBORATED/CREDIBLE). Benchmark every plan against Knopp's 92-plan normal ranges (wg2 §2, CORROBORATED as description, not efficacy).
  4. History import makes the safety rules computable (C7, C11). The primary load rule needs "longest run in the last 30 days" (Frandsen 2025, wg4 §1, CORROBORATED). Imported FIT/GPX history supplies it reliably (FIT is the de facto format: hearing-1 sys, CREDIBLE). The data model must treat imported history as a primary input, with a manual fallback.
  5. Multi-year continuity (C3, C4). Competence levels must carry across plans. Maintenance mode, at ≥2 sessions/week with intensity kept, holds VO2max for up to about 15 weeks (wg1 §5, CORROBORATED). Durability is UNKNOWN. Seasons can chain without restarting from zero, provided the plan says the long-run base must be rebuilt.

2. Biggest risk: overclaimed upside

No RCT or cohort compares adaptive with static plans (factdesk1 §5, UNKNOWN). Runna's "43% fewer injuries" is vendor-reported (ALLEGED). Claiming "prevents injury" would repeat that error. The guard: every user-facing benefit statement carries a claim ceiling equal to its evidence grade. The bigger opportunity: this method can produce the missing evidence (C13).

3. Concrete proposal: Calibration & Evidence Loop module

  • Prediction record. Each plan stores predicted_band (point estimate ±6–8% minimum, wg1 implication 5), inputs used, and model version.
  • Personal calibration. After each race or test, compute residual = actual/predicted − 1. The personal_factor is the median of the last ≤3 residuals, clamped to ±the current band. Design choice, no evidence for the clamp. It may adjust paces and feasibility only. It may never raise volume or relax any safety cap.
  • Outcome log (opt-in, anonymised). Completion, sessions done/planned, self-reported injury (site, days lost), illness, red-flag triggers, prediction error.
  • Pre-registered metrics for C13, compared only as context and not as targets:
  • prediction MAE against published 5.9–8% (wg1 §7);
  • completion against the 27.3% C25K baseline (different population, caveat);
  • injury per 1000 h against Videbæk's 7.7/17.8 (wg4 §2).
  • Harness test. CI fails if any generated plan breaches a safety cap. It also warns when weekly km, longest run or TID falls outside the Knopp ranges for the matching tier.

4. Information requests

  1. Fact desk: are the Vickers & Vertosick 2016 model coefficients (results + weekly volume) published in usable form, so the feasibility engine can implement them rather than Riegel?
  2. Fact desk: does any controlled study show run-walk or ≤3-day plans raise completion or adherence in novices, beyond finish time and muscle pain?
opening-firstprinciples.md

Opening — First Principles (firstprinciples)

Purpose: close the gap between what the runner can do now and what the goal demands, within the time they have, without injury or drop-out. What matters most: consistent volume, the long run, an honest goal and safety gates. Fine detail matters less. Intensity-distribution effects are small and the zone-setting method mattered little (wg1 §2, CORROBORATED). No load model is valid for prescription (ACWR CONTRADICTED; hearing-1).

1. Requirements

  1. Every element must change a decision (C4, C10). A node, input or parameter that alters no session, gate or feasibility verdict is explanation, not engine. "Mental skills" has no dose evidence, so it is content, not a gate.
  2. Small core state (C2):
  • weekly running time
  • longest run in the last 30 days
  • a dated race or critical-speed (CS) anchor
  • sessions per week
  • risk flags

Never anchor zones on an HR formula (error ±10–12 bpm; wg1 §4, CORROBORATED).

  1. Fixed conflict order (C5, C8):
  2. safety gates
  3. feasibility
  4. long-run spike cap (wg4 §1, CORROBORATED, observational)
  5. frequency (wg3 §2, CORROBORATED, cycling)
  6. volume (wg1 §1, CORROBORATED association)
  7. key sessions
  8. TID preset
  9. One mechanism for all distances and horizons (C1, C3). Distance changes only the demand vector. Duration changes only how many phases fit.
  10. Honest adaptation (C6). Adaptation means re-measure and re-plan, never "prevents injury". Adaptive vs static plans is UNKNOWN (factdesk1 §5), and graded plans were not protective (GRONORUN, wg4, CORROBORATED).

2. Biggest risk: precision as decoration

Paces to the second, CTL ramps, monotony scores and seven-level trees look rigorous but rest on ALLEGED numbers (monotony >2.0, deload ratios, Daniels' return bands) and bury the real levers.

Guard:

  • Tag every parameter evidence or design-default, with its grade.
  • Run a removal test in simulation (C13): delete each rule in turn and regenerate the persona plans. If no plan changes materially, the rule becomes display-only.

3. Proposal: the gap-closing loop

State S = {V: 7- and 28-day running time; L: longest run in the last 30 days; P: dated anchor pace; F: sessions per week; R: flags}.

Demand D(distance, target) = {V_req, L_req, P_req, specific readiness, fuelling readiness if the race lasts over 90 min (wg5, CORROBORATED)}. V_req and L_req are design defaults, benchmarked on the Knopp plan corpus (43/59/108 km/wk; longest run 31–35 km). The corpus is CORROBORATED as what plans prescribe, not as proof that they work.

Weekly steps:

  1. Apply the gates (wg4).
  2. Compute the gap G = D − S.
  3. Feasibility: add up three things:
  • weeks to close L, with each new longest run ≤ 1.10 × L30;
  • weeks to close V under a design-default ceiling;
  • the taper: 2 weeks, or 3 for the marathon (wg2 §3, CORROBORATED).

If the total is more than the time available, offer a later date, a shorter distance or a slower target (C9).

  1. Allocate, in this order: frequency, then long run, then volume, then 1–3 key sessions. Take them from the competence node with the largest gap whose prerequisites are met.
  2. Phase follows the dominant gap: base when V or L dominates; build or specific when P or specific readiness dominates; a fixed taper window before the race. This covers 4 weeks to 4 years without per-horizon templates.

Adaptation reruns the loop on the updated S. Illness, pain and bone stress injury stay as gates.

4. Requests

  1. At 5 h/week or less, do recreational runners gain more performance per hour from extra volume or from extra intensity? The answer sets the allocation order.
  2. Is Frandsen's spike risk independent of weekly volume? Does it hold for time on feet and for run-walk? The answer decides whether L can be the main load-safety variable.
opening-outsider.md

Opening — Outsider (first-time self-coached runner)

My reader has no coach, no lab test and no race time, and does not know "threshold", "VDOT", "Z2" or "CTL".

1. What the method must get right

  1. Beginners can answer every input, and "I don't know" is allowed (crit. 2, 12). Pace anchors need a recent race or a critical-speed test (factdesk1 §3, CORROBORATED). Risk flags are plain facts the user knows (WG3 §2/§5, CORROBORATED). Every input has a named default for "I don't know", shown to the user.
  2. Effort words come before numbers (crit. 5, 12). Talk test and RPE are valid markers for the first and second ventilatory thresholds (factdesk1 §3, CORROBORATED). Age-formula heart-rate zones are off by about ±10–12 bpm (WG1 impl. 4, CORROBORATED). The default easy instruction is "you can still speak in full sentences".
  3. Safety text works without a clinician (crit. 7). Write red flags as actions, for example "Stop. Do not run. See a doctor today." Name symptoms (chest pain, fainting, bone pain worsening with load; WG4 §4, CORROBORATED), not "BSI". The pain rule (≤3/10, settled within 24 h: factdesk1 §2, design default) needs a 0–10 scale with words at each anchor.
  4. Translate the evidence grades (crit. 8, 12). Users see "well supported / reasonable practice / coach's convention", mapped one-to-one to the internal grades.
  5. Feasibility in plain consequences (crit. 9). Riegel is too optimistic for the marathon (WG3 §4, CORROBORATED). Show "likely too ambitious in 10 weeks; options: X or Y", not a falsely precise time.

2. Biggest risk: jargon turns guesses into false precision

A novice guesses a field they don't understand (a 10K time, a formula HRmax). The engine then turns it into exact paces that look authoritative. VDOT validation is UNKNOWN (factdesk1 §3), and C25K completion is only 27% (WG3 §5, CORROBORATED). Guard: every input gets a confidence flag (measured / estimated / unknown). Paces come only from measured anchors; otherwise the plan prescribes effort. A beginner's first 2–4 weeks are effort-only and end with a simple field test that sets the anchor (design choice).

3. Proposal: a "plain-language contract" module, enforced by tests

  • All user-facing text comes from a message catalogue. Each message has plain_text, why (one sentence) and coach_rationale (grade plus source).
  • Lint rule: a glossary term (VO2max, threshold, taper, deload, zones, RPE, ACWR, CTL…) is either replaced by a plain word or linked to the glossary on first use. An unlisted technical term fails the build.
  • Effort ladder as the shared vocabulary: walk / easy (full sentences) / steady (short phrases) / hard (a few words) / very hard (no talking). It maps internally to zones.
  • Comprehension test (crit. 13): 5–8 first-time runners read one generated week plus the red-flag card. Without help, they answer "what do I run tomorrow, how hard, and when must I stop?" Pass at ≥80% (design choice).

4. Requests

  1. Are the talk test and RPE valid for novices and during run-walk intervals? The 2022 review covers general populations. If they are not, the beginner easy-run anchor is unsupported.
  2. How reliable are recreational runners' self-reported race times and HRmax? Vickers used self-report (WG3 §4). The answer decides whether "estimated" inputs may drive paces.
opening-statusquo.md

Opening — Status-quo advocate (statusquo)

Proven plans share a core: mostly easy running, 1–3 key sessions, a long run, rising specificity and a taper (hearing-1 coach, COR). No school has beaten another head to head (COR). No adaptive app has beaten a static plan on injury or adherence (factdesk1 §5, COR that the evidence is absent). The honest added value is a safety layer, fitting the plan to any duration or schedule, and principled re-planning.

1. Requirements

  • R1 — The shared core is invariant (C5, C10). Every plan keeps about 80% easy running with a pyramidal default (wg1 #2; wg2 §2 Knopp, COR), 0–3 key sessions, a long run, and a taper that keeps intensity and frequency (wg2 §3 Bosquet + Smyth & Lawlor, COR).
  • R2 — Each school is a whole, coherent preset (C8). Hansons' 16-mile cap only works with 6 days and 3 SOS sessions. Daniels' min(25–30%, 150 min) assumes enough volume. Higdon's 20-miler assumes 4 days and no quality work (wg2 §1, COR/CRED). A hybrid that takes a cap from one school and a frequency from another must be explicit and logged.
  • R3 — Adaptation is bounded edits, not re-optimisation (C6). No load model is validated for prescription (hearing-1 phys: ACWR CONTRA, Banister descriptive only). Allowed edits: repeat a week, drop or swap a quality session, shorten the long run, shift a phase, re-anchor paces. The skeleton survives unless the goal changes.
  • R4 — Keep what works for beginners (C3, C12). Run-walk is a first-class preset (Galloway, COR). Weeks can be repeated. Instructions stay minimal: completion is about 27% (wg3 #4, COR).
  • R5 — The value-add is measured against the parent template (C13), not against nothing.

2. Biggest risk: the "Frankenplan"

Free generation produces plans that look evidence-based but are combinations no runner has ever followed. Safety rules can also conflict with practice. Take a 40 km/week novice: the Daniels cap gives a long run far shorter than popular plans use, yet a cohort associated long runs over 21 km with faster half-marathon times (factdesk1 §7, COR, associational). Guard: generate from a template first, keep a deviation ledger, and test as in §3.

3. Proposal: a conformance-and-deviation test module

  1. Fixtures. Encode skeletons of Higdon Novice 1, Hansons, Pfitzinger 18/55, Daniels 2Q, Galloway and a Koop-ordered ultra, using only parameters graded CORROBORATED or better.
  2. Forward test. With matching inputs, the output must match the fixture's invariants within tolerance. The invariants are volume-curve shape, long-run peak, quality sessions per week, taper weeks and intensity distribution. Any departure must emit {rule, criterion, grade, delta}.
  3. Reverse test. Run the safety overlay on each fixture: the 1.10× single-session cap (wg4 #2, COR) and the long-run presets. A flag on a widely used plan is either a recalibration finding or a user-facing warning, never a silent fix.
  4. Range check. Flag outputs outside the Knopp ranges (43/59/108 km/week, longest run 31–35 km) for review. These ranges are not targets.

The tolerance is a design choice, defaulting to ±10% on volume and long-run peak, and exact on quality-session count and taper weeks.

4. Requests

  1. What are the primary-book long-run sequences for Higdon Novice 1, Hansons and Pfitzinger 18/55, and do they satisfy the 1.10× cap (gap #19)?
  2. Is there any completion or injury data for runners who follow a published static plan as written? (C13 pilot baseline)
opening-systemsarchitect.md

Opening — Systems Architect (systemsarchitect)

1. What the method must get right

R1. Fixed rule precedence, no ties (C5, C11). Rule engines fire equal-priority rules in "arbitrary or first-found" order (hearing-1 sys-field, CORROBORATED). Every rule belongs to exactly one tier, applied in this order:

  • S0 red flags
  • S1 screening gates
  • H hard limits
  • F feasibility
  • P structure
  • D preferences

Each decision table declares a DMN hit policy (hearing-1 sys-acad, CREDIBLE). A lower tier never overrides a higher one.

R2. Evidence travels with every rule (C8). Each rule records its grade, its sources and its basis: evidence, convention or design_default. A rule resting only on ALLEGED support may block only as a labelled conservative default. Example: pain ≤3/10 is a design default, because the published thresholds are CONTRADICTED (factdesk1 §2).

R3. Re-planning is a pure function (C6). plan = f(profile, goal, log, date, rule version), with no hidden state. LLM plans vary from run to run (hearing-1 sys, CORROBORATED); this method must not. Pace changes need a consistent pattern across sessions, not one outlier (Runna, sys-field, CORROBORATED). That hysteresis prevents oscillation.

R4. The competence graph is a checked DAG (C4, C10). There is no precedent for such a graph (sys-acad, UNKNOWN), so correctness must come from machine checks:

  • the graph is acyclic;
  • every module declares its prerequisite levels;
  • every goal node is reachable within the plan's duration.

R5. Invariants are testable on any plan (C13).

  • A session longer than 1.10 × the longest run of the last 30 days warns; above 2.0 × it is blocked (WG4, CORROBORATED, observational).
  • The long run stays within the preset cap (WG2, CREDIBLE as a choice).
  • The taper keeps intensity and frequency (WG1, CORROBORATED).
  • ACWR is never used as a gate (WG4, CORROBORATED).
  • Age caps hold (WG4).

2. Biggest risk: modules that silently conflict

Each module is sensible on its own, but together they can contradict each other:

  • Feasibility wants a 32 km long run, which the spike cap and the long-run cap forbid. Higdon's 20 mi run at about 40 mi/wk already breaks the Daniels cap (WG2).
  • Deload weeks fight ramp targets.

If firing order decides these conflicts, plans become nondeterministic or unsafe. And "deterministic ≠ protective" (sys-crit, CREDIBLE): weak thresholds stay weak even when they are perfectly testable.

Guard: H-tier rules are constraints. If they cannot all be met, the output is an explicit infeasibility with ranked repairs (move the date → lower the goal → shorten the distance; C3, C9), never a silently violated limit. Coaches see the grade of every threshold.

3. Proposal: rule record plus generate → validate → repair

Each rule is stored as one record:

{id, version, tier, scope, inputs[], condition, action, hit_policy, evidence{grade, sources[], basis}, explain{runner, coach}, tests[]}

The pipeline:

  1. Generate: from phase templates and the P tier.
  2. Validate: an independent validator checks every H rule and every R5 invariant.
  3. Repair: apply the declared actions (cap, redistribute, extend phase), up to N passes. N is a design default.
  4. Fail closed: if the plan still fails, return infeasibility.

Build-time static checks:

  • overlapping conditions within a tier;
  • unreachable rules;
  • cycles;
  • ungraded parameters.

Regression: golden outputs for the worked personas plus edge cases (≤4 weeks, 3 h/wk, 12 h/wk, masters, return from injury). Plans are also compared against Knopp's ranges (WG2, CORROBORATED), as flags rather than targets.

4. Information requests

  1. Frandsen measured spikes by distance only (WG4). Does any evidence support applying the cap to duration, for run-walk and time-based plans? The answer fixes the canonical unit of the main invariant.
  2. Owner: will the site ingest device logs (FIT) or only self-reported completion? The 30-day longest run and pattern-based adaptation both depend on recorded history.

Rooms

d1-roomA.md

Design room A — sub-council round 1 (5K–10K family), artifact v1

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

Expansionist: Short races are where most users start (C25K completion 27.3%, wg3 §5, CORROBORATED). If the novice path fails, reach fails. Status-quo: The novice path is already out of line with practice. Persona P01 (zero history, 5K in 10 weeks) is expected "feasible or stretch". The rules make it infeasible. Systems Architect: Worse, 05 §3 puts L_req inside the long-run min(). For a 5K, the long run is capped at race distance. Easy runs are capped at the long run (LOD-031), so volume falls. TOL drops below L2 and SES-M-010 becomes ineligible, yet a 5K time goal requires VO2 L2. That is a deadlock. Context Realist: The TID cap (E3+E4 ≤ 10%) comes from Knopp's corpus. Those are 12-week sub-elite plans with 31–35 km long runs (wg2 §2, CORROBORATED as description), so the cap is used outside its envelope. At 180 min/week it allows 18 min of hard work, and the THR L2 exit test needs 25 min. Status-quo: FIRST needs exactly 3 runs and 3 quality sessions. LOD-033 would quietly shorten all three. That is the Frankenplan we banned. Expansionist: The target time never sets paces (INV-15; RESOLUTIONS D2). So why does TARGET_OUTSIDE_BAND block a plan? It even blocks a 2-year goal, against FEA-024. Context Realist: Agreed, provided we never project gains (factdesk1 §6, UNKNOWN). Keep the block when there is no time to re-test (≤8 weeks). Systems Architect: Every fix must stay deterministic. Interim targets and checkpoints have to be registry values.

Case walk-throughs

  1. P01: novice, no history, 5K finish in 10 weeks, 3 days/week. GEN-012 → return ladder from R1: rtr = 5 weeks. G = 7 weeks (30 → 44 min long run at 1.10×, deload every 3). Slack = 10 − (5+4+1+2+2) = −4 → infeasible, earliest date ≈ 16 weeks. Problems: BUILD and SPECIFIC hold no quality sessions while HAB < L2 (SES-007) yet use up weeks. The ladder doubles running minutes from R1 to R2 because INV-02 counts walk time. HAB L2 needs continuous running, so a run-walk finisher can never reach it, and HAB sits in neither K nor G. Two ladders exist (rtr.ladder, ses.runwalk.ladder).
  2. 45-year-old, 3 runs, 25 km (~160 min)/week, 10K 58:00 measured, wants sub-55 in 10 weeks. TOL L2, AER L1. V_req = 180 min (AER L2). K = 8 weeks ≤ 8 → fits. P_race 56:50, fast edge 52:52 → stretch. The long run is capped at L_req = 8 km/60 min, below the runner's current 65 min. With 1 quality slot (TOL ≤ L2), CMP-007 picks THR every week, and THR L2 cannot be tested under the 10% cap. Output: 10 weeks of threshold intervals, with no race-pace or VO2 work.
  3. 32-year-old, 4 runs, 30 km/week, 5K 24:00, wants 22:30 in 12 weeks. Stretch. The long run is capped near 31 min. Easy runs are capped at 31 min, so V falls from ~170 to ~138 min. TOL drops to L1 and VO2 intervals become ineligible. Deadlock (above).
  4. Experienced runner new to the app, 10K time goal in 3 weeks (H1). Self-reported THR/VO2/MSP are capped at L1 (CMP-004), so FEA-023 gives CURRENT_STATE_SHORT → no plan. The only option is "finish", effort-only. SHARPEN holds volume, so blocking prevents no risk.
  5. Sub-20 5K in 2 years from 23:00 (H5). FEA-032 → TARGET_OUTSIDE_BAND → infeasible, which contradicts FEA-024 ("cap at stretch").
  6. FIRST preset, 3 runs + 2 cross-training, 10K. DST-016 checks days and time only. LOD-033 shortens all three key runs, and the result is still labelled FIRST.

Required changes

  1. 05 §3 LOD-010 / LR(w): drop L_req from the min() for family = short. L_req stays a floor (INV-25). Room B should check road_classic. Why: this is the deadlock in cases 2 and 3. The long run serves AER/DUR (DST-005; hearing-1 §2, CORROBORATED). C5, C4, C10.
  2. 05 LOD-033 + 03 FEA-020/021: make tid.hard_share_max family-scoped, with the short-family value marked PROVISIONAL until IR-1. The FEA dry run must check that each required exit-test dose fits the hard-share allowance at V_peak; if not, return LEVELS_UNREACHABLE. Why: the cap comes from marathon plans, so short races are outside its envelope (wg2 §2). Exit tests are currently unreachable without any notice. C5, C8, C9.
  3. 02 CMP-007 / 09 DST-002: for time goals in SPECIFIC, once the MSP L1 prerequisites hold, schedule an MSP session at least every dst.msp_every_weeks (2, design default). Rotation stays deterministic. Why: rising specificity (hearing-1 §2, CORROBORATED) is lost with 1 slot. C4, C10.
  4. 04 §3 + 03 §4: for 5K/10K finish goals that add no THR/VO2/MSP levels, set build_min = specific_min = 0 and taper.weeks.short_finish = 1 (design default). Fix P01's expected outcome and add a Couch-to-5K fixture to TST-060. Why: empty phases inflate weeks_needed (case 1). C3, C9, C11.
  5. 02 §2 HAB L2 + 06 §3: for finish goals with run_walk_preference, HAB L2 is attained by run-walk sessions of at least the expected race duration, 3 per week. State how HAB enters weeks_needed. Why: run-walk is meant to be first-class (wg2 §1, Galloway/Hottenrott, CORROBORATED). C2, C4.
  6. 02 §5 vs parameters feas.finish_floor: the 10K finish requirement (AER L2 = 180 min) overrides the 90-min floor. Keep one value (proposed: AER L1 + DUR L1). C5, C9.
  7. 03 FEA-023 / 04 PHS-010 (H1): do not use non-volume levels as feasibility gates. SHARPEN uses intensity modules whose prerequisites hold, including at self-reported levels, with ≤ 1 quality session if none is logged. The target is judged against the band only. C3, C9.
  8. 03 FEA-032 (with FEA-024): TARGET_OUTSIDE_BAND stays infeasible in H1–H2 only. In H3–H5 it becomes the stretch reason TARGET_BEYOND_CURRENT, with an interim target at the band edge and a field test every pred.checkpoint_weeks (6, design default). No gain is projected. Why: the target sets neither paces nor load (INV-15, ADP-064, RESOLUTIONS D2), and FEA-024 and FEA-032 conflict. C3, C9, C5.
  9. 09 DST-016 + 06 §5: check TID as part of preset compatibility. FIRST either gets a preset-scoped hard share from its encoded fixture or is declared incompatible; it is never silently gutted. Add FIRST to TST-060. C8, C10.

Optional improvements

  • One ladder for novices and returners, with a cap on running minutes per rung (labelled design default). Show "start ladder" wording to people who have never run.
  • CTX-006 at exactly 3 runs: method_default allows ≤ 1 quality session (ALLEGED, wg1 impl. 2).
  • A labelled 2-run/week 5K finish option instead of AVAILABILITY_DAYS (the floor is ALLEGED).
  • A tighter band for same-distance anchors (IR-3).

Criteria check for this focus

  • C2 Partly: run-walk is not first-class at HAB L2.
  • C3 Partly: H1 and H5 time goals are broken.
  • C4 Partly: the TOL/VO2 deadlock.
  • C5 Partly: the L_req cap, TID vs exit tests, FEA-024 vs FEA-032.
  • C6 Met.
  • C7 Met.
  • C8 Partly: the TID cap is used outside its envelope.
  • C9 Partly: novice and short-horizon verdicts are wrong.
  • C10 Partly: FIRST, MSP placement.
  • C11 Partly: P01 contradicts the rules, and 13 is missing.
  • C12 Met.
  • C13 Partly: no short-race fixtures.

Information requests (fact desk)

  1. What share of weekly time is at threshold or faster in published 5K/10K plans at 3–5 runs/week, and what is their longest run relative to race distance?
  2. What is the week-by-week structure of Couch-to-5K (running minutes per session)? What are the 8–16-week improvement rates for novice and recreational 5K/10K runners (e.g. RCT control or intervention arms)?
  3. What is the test-retest variability of 5K/10K times in recreational runners (to set a band for same-distance anchors)?

Expert requests: (a) Coaching practice (field): a sanity check of the default 3-run short-race week. (b) Sports medicine: is a 5-week ladder from 1:2 run-walk to 30 min continuous defensible for true novices? (c) Knowledge modelling: the cost of session-level checks inside the feasibility dry run.

NO. The architecture holds. The failures are parameter and rule-coupling defects that can be fixed inside the tiers.

d1-roomB.md

D1 Room B — Half & marathon (sub-council round 1)

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

Systems Architect: The provisional default fails worse than RESOLUTIONS says. With the 10% planning margin (03 §2), anyone with an easy pace over 6:24/km breaks 150 min even at ratio 2.00. higdon_novice fails its own cap: 42.195/1.30 = 32.46 km > lr.abs_cap_km 32. galloway and first declare no caps at all (LOD-020 table). The half is broken too: 16.2 km at a 30% share needs 54 km/week. Contrarian: I came in for Option A, an honest failure. But the share caps have no injury evidence (hearing-1 §2, UNKNOWN). The spike cap does (wg4 §1, CORROBORATED, observational). If the default refuses, users go to Higdon's published sequence, where 7 of 9 steps exceed 1.10× (factdesk2 §7, ALLEGED sequence). I accept relaxing the share cap only if T2 stays untouched and runs over 150 min must be fuelled. Outsider: "No plan" for a runner doing 30 km/week and 4 runs reads as a broken app. A 3-hour longest run is easy to explain. A 32 km run at 7:30/km (4 h) is not. Context Realist: Slow runners are where it bites. A time cap keeps their long runs bounded. A distance cap does not. The 4-runs/week requirement for the half has no source behind it. Architect: Then use one preset record, with time as the unit and an explicit floor, so the rules stay deterministic.

d1-roomC.md

Design room C, sub-council round 1: 50–100 km road/flat ultras (artifact v1)

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

First Principles: The ultra generator decides four things: the peak long run (L_req), the peak weekly volume (V_req), how to reach both safely, and whether they fit the runner's week. The only corroborated single-session limit is the spike cap of 1.10 × L30_eff [wg4 §1, CORROBORATED, distance-only, not ultra runners]. Long-run share caps have no injury evidence [hearing-1 §2, UNKNOWN]. The % cap should therefore shape the week. It should not drive the demand.

Contrarian: At 0.40, the % cap drives the demand. In FEA-001, V_req = L_req / pct. A 50K then needs 78 km/week and a 100K needs 100 km/week. The Comrades finish tier is 45–50 km/week for about 89 km [wg2 §4, CORROBORATED as description]. The provisional value fails runners that practice routinely finishes. My worry runs the other way too: raising the cap "so plans pass" is designing to the answer. The long run must not swallow a low-volume week.

Expansionist: For 100 km, volume is the lever [wg1 §1, wg2 §4, CORROBORATED association]. Koop's plateau is about 9 h/week [hearing-1 §2, CREDIBLE], yet the provisional default makes a 100K on 9 h/week infeasible. Back-to-backs could let slow runners build time on feet with smaller single sessions.

Contrarian: Back-to-backs are ALLEGED, with no trial [wg1 §6], and the two-day risk is UNKNOWN. They cannot be the main tool. Option C (an absolute distance cap) penalises slow runners, and its sources are mostly trail [wg2 §4].

Systems Architect: Three defects show up whatever we pick:

  1. FEA's growth formula assumes one long-run step per week. DST-009 places a peak only every lr.ultra_peak_every_weeks weeks, so feasibility overstates speed by about 2×.
  2. The DST-012 tune-up (50 km for a 100K) can never pass INV-01 on a plan that peaks at 40 km (1.10 × 40 = 44), and it needs about 125 km/week under the 0.40 cap. The rule is dead.
  3. knopp.range.ultra has no 50K row, so DST-014 is undefined for the 50K.

First Principles: Here is the proposal. Put the % cap on the peak week only. Let the existing 0.70 alternation keep the two-week share near 0.50. That value is Higdon's widely used convention [wg2 §1, CORROBORATED as practice]. When the time cap binds, treat it as a time-on-feet target, not a failure. Time is already the planning unit [05 §1; wg2 §1].

Contrarian: I accept it if the label says "no injury data" and the time-cap substitution caps the verdict at stretch. I record a dissent: I would still prefer fail-closed at the time cap. Expansionist and Systems Architect agree with the proposal.

Case walk-throughs

Case 1: first 50K from a marathon background on 6 h/week. The runner is 34, with a 3:50 marathon, 40 km/week on 4 runs, L30 = 22 km, 360 min/week, a race in 18 weeks, a finish goal, and S_slow = 6:45/km.

  • Provisional default: L_req = 31.25 km (211 min). V_req_time = 211 / 0.40 = 528 min, which is over 360, so AVAILABILITY_TIME → infeasible.
  • Recommended: V_req_time = 352 min and V_req_dist = max(50, 52) = 52 km, so the goal is feasible with LOW_SLACK.
  • Growth: 4 long-run peaks × 2 weeks + 2 deloads = 10 weeks.
  • Peak week: about 52 km, with a 31 km long run (60%) and three runs of about 7 km.
  • Two-day budget: about 6 km on each day next to the long run.
  • Fuelling: a 30 → 60–90 g/h ladder over about 10 practice runs fits. The EAH card shows for the race (over 4 h).
  • Problem: the long run is more than 4× each of the other runs. This is labelled, and it matches novice-marathon practice.

Case 2: first 100K on 9 h/week. The runner is 41, with a 3:30 marathon and one 50K done, 65 km/week on 5 runs, L30 = 30 km, 540 min/week, 20 weeks, and S_slow = 6:25/km.

  • Provisional default: V_req = 100 km and 642 min → infeasible. This contradicts the Koop plateau.
  • Recommended: L_req = 40 km (257 min). V_req = 66.7 km (428 min) → feasible.
  • Growth: n_L = 4 peaks × 2 weeks + deloads = 10 weeks.
  • Problems:
  • The tune-up is always replaced (fixed in R4).
  • About 110 min/week of available time goes unused, even though volume is the lever (O1).
  • DST-014 flags 67 km against the publishers' 90–120 km/week; the Bronze tier is 50–65 km/week.

Case 3: masters 100K, hot race. The runner is 58, with a 4:05 marathon, 55 km/week on 5 runs, L30 = 28 km, 480 min/week, 24 weeks, and S_slow = 7:40/km.

  • Deloads: the masters context gives deload.every = 3.
  • Demand: L_req = 40 km (307 min, under the 330 cap). V_req_time = 307 / 0.60 = 512 min, which is over 480, so the result is AVAILABILITY_TIME → infeasible by 32 min. Ranked options: add 35 min/week, or choose the 50K (feasible).
  • Problem: S_slow converts the whole week at the slow planning pace, which is conservative. The outcome is acceptable but borderline.
  • Heat: HEAT L2 adds 7–14 exposure days inside 21 days before the race, the plan runs on effort, and the EAH card shows.
  • Gap: no rule touches a 5 h peak long run on a hot day (O3).
  • Variant at S_slow = 8:45/km: L_req_time = 350 min, over the 330 cap. Under v1 this fails as LONG_RUN_UNREACHABLE_PRESET. Under R2 it becomes a 330-min target, stretch, and the runner is warned about race cut-offs (O2).

Required changes

R1. OPEN-2 decision (parameters §7; 09 DST-008; 05 LOD-020).

  • lr.ultra_pct_cap = 0.60 of V(w), range 0.50–0.70. It applies only in weeks with a peak long run. Non-peak weeks are already held by dst.ultra_alt_long_ratio = 0.70 (kept), which makes the two-week long-run share about 0.51.
  • lr.ultra_time_cap_min = 330, range 240–360 (kept).
  • lr.ultra_peak_every_weeks = 2 (kept).
  • Both caps apply under all presets when family = ultra. Remove the PROVISIONAL labels.
  • Grade: design default, bracketed by practice: 100K plans about 0.35 [wg2 §4, ALLEGED/CREDIBLE], Higdon 0.50 [wg2 §1, CORROBORATED as practice], Comrades tune-up about 1.0 [wg2 §4, CREDIBLE].
  • Runner label: "Ultra long-run cap: up to 60% of a peak week and 5 h 30. Coaching convention; no injury data exist for road ultras."
  • Options B, C and D are rejected (see the session). Back-to-backs stay opt-in (DST-010).
  • Criteria served: C9, C7, C8.

R2. 03 FEA-003 (ultra branch). If L_req_time > lr.ultra_time_cap_min, set L_req to the cap ("330 min or 40 km, whichever comes first") and add the stretch reason LONG_RUN_TIME_CAPPED. Do not mark the goal infeasible. The spike cap still binds, and demand only goes down. Criteria served: C9, C3, C5.

R3. 03 §3 growth formula. For family = ultra, use n_L × lr.ultra_peak_every_weeks. Criteria served: C9, C5.

R4. 09 DST-012 and 07 INV-13.

  • Tune-up distance = min(dst.tuneup_dist_km, round_down(1.10 × planned L30_eff)).
  • The tune-up is exempt from lr.ultra_pct_cap and lr.ultra_time_cap_min. It is never exempt from INV-01, INV-02 or INV-03.
  • The tune-up counts as that block's peak long run.
  • Criteria served: C10, C5, C7.

R5. 09 DST-009 and 05 LOD-040. A deload week counts as a non-peak week, and peak cadence restarts after it. Criteria served: C5, C11.

R6. 09 DST-014 and parameters knopp.range.ultra. The tiers apply to 100K only. DST-014 is silent for the 50K because no source covers it. Criteria served: C5, C8.

R7. 03 FEA-001. For family = ultra, V_req_dist = max(feas.finish_floor, L_req_dist / lr.ultra_pct_cap). Add test fixtures for the three cases above (14). Criteria served: C11, C13.

Optional improvements

  • O1. Ultra "volume-first" option (ask_confirm): raise V_peak toward the goal tier within the time the runner has, under LOD-003. Label it as an association only [wg1 §1].
  • O2. Add a Goal.cutoff_h field and warn when the high end of ultra_range exceeds it.
  • O3. For a long run of 180 min or more forecast hot, offer an earlier start or the non-peak (0.70) value, labelled as a design default [wg5 §4].
  • O4. Report a marginal AVAILABILITY_TIME result (within 10%) with the exact number of minutes to add.
  • Cross-room flag to room B and the plenary. Marathon caps (150 min / 25%) against ultra caps (330 min / 60%) create a cliff between families. A runner could enter a 50K just to unlock longer runs ("goal-shopping"). OPEN-1 should be resolved with this in view.

d2-roomA.md

Design room A — sub-council round 2 (5K–10K family), artifact v2

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

Systems Architect: All 9 of room A's round-1 changes were applied (RESOLUTIONS A-R1…R9). A-R3 has two faults. 13 P2 puts an MSP session in week 11, which is taper, so it should be week 9. And an H2 plan's merged BUILD/SPECIFIC phase has no Phase.type, so DST-018 is undetermined there. Contrarian: N-02. The 5-week ladder is a return-to-run protocol (factdesk1 §1) applied to people who have never run. Running time doubles from R1 to R2, and INV-02 counts walking, so it cannot catch that. Tested programmes run 8–13 weeks (GRONORUN, C25K). Five weeks is outside that range. First Principles: With no evidence either way, take the default people already use. But C25K's own goal (5K finish in 10 weeks) must stay feasible, so the 5K-finish demand must equal the C25K exit. Outsider: A beginner understands "follows the NHS 9-week programme". Drop-out has not been linked to ramp speed (hearing-2 med-crit), so the label must promise nothing either way. Systems Architect: N-03/N-04. At 3 runs, "easy ≤ LR" plus the short-race share cap of 0.30 cannot both hold: 0.60V ≥ 0.70V is impossible. V freezes at about 2 × LR + Q, and the FEA dry run never checks that. Contrarian: Let the long run carry volume in 1.10× steps (wg4 §1, CORROBORATED), but only up to demand. Growing toward V_next turns a 10K plan into a mileage chase. First Principles: Reject "an easy run of 1.2 × LR". That is just the long run under another name. Contrarian: The 0.12 polarized override is backwards. The polarized trial arms had the most zone 3 (hearing-2 coach-acad).

Case walk-throughs

  1. P1: novice, 5K finish in 10 weeks, 3 days/week. v2 output: stretch, with 30 min continuous reached by week 4, about twice C25K's pace. With a 9-week ladder alone and the 1.30 ratio (L_req 34.2 min), the plan needs 12 weeks, so it is infeasible, which is nonsense. With R1–R2: 9 ladder weeks + race week → stretch (LOW_SLACK, ESTIMATED_INPUTS).
  2. Woman, 45, 3 runs (Tue/Thu/Sun), 160 min/week, L30 65 min, 10K in 58:00 measured, wants sub-55 in 10 weeks. Fast edge ≈ 52:52 → stretch. V_req = 180 (THR L2 needs AER L2). The fill gives LR 65 (held), Q ≈ 38, easy ≤ 65, so V ≈ 168. AER L2 and THR L2 are never reached, yet the plan is issued: silent failure. With R3: LR 71 (≤ 1.10×) gives V = 180, long-run share 0.39.
  3. P2: 4 runs, 10K in 48:00. V is 221 against V_next 300; demand is met, so a message is enough (N-04). The MSP week is wrong.
  4. Advanced man, 29, 6 runs, 420 min/week, 10K in 38:00 measured 3 weeks ago, target 36:45 in 6 weeks (H2). P_race 37:14, 7% fast edge 34:38 → stretch. A 35:00 target (7.9% in 6 weeks) would also be "stretch", although trained retest noise is 1–1.5% (factdesk3 §3, CORROBORATED). TID 11%. DST-018 is undetermined (no phase type).
  5. Experienced runner new to the app, 10K in 3 weeks, claimed L30 70 min. L30_eff 63 ≥ L_req ≈ 62 → SHARPEN, feasible. A-R7 verified.

Required changes

  1. 10 §4, SAF-080/085, parameters rtr.start_ladder (N-02). Entry "no history, training age 0" → a 9-rung start ladder paced to NHS C25K: 3 clean sessions per rung, no rung skipped, so ≥ 9 weeks. Running minutes per rung follow the published schedule (IR-1). Returners keep the 5-rung ladder. Label: "convention; tested programmes run 8–13 weeks with similar injury rates (GRONORUN, C25K, CORROBORATED); shorter is untested". C7, C8, C10.
  2. 03 §2, 02 §2, parameters race.5k_finish_lr_min = 30. For 5K finish goals, the long-run demand is 30 min (continuous, or run-walk under the A-R5 rule). The 1.30 ratio stays for time goals. Basis: C25K, as for F-18 (factdesk3 §2, week 9 CREDIBLE). Completing the start ladder credits HAB L2 for a 5K finish. C9, C3.
  3. 05 LOD-010/031, parameters lr.pct_cap.short (N-03/N-04). When water-filling binds below max(V_req, V_hold), raise LR(w) along the LOD-003 curve under INV-01/02 and the time cap. Never do this for V_next. Set the short-race share cap by run count: 0.45 at ≤ 3 runs, 0.40 at 4, 0.30 at ≥ 5 (design default; the link between share and injury is UNKNOWN, factdesk3 §7). A V_next shortfall gets MSG-LOD-FILL ("add a run day to grow"). Keep "easy ≤ LR". C5, C4, C12.
  4. 03 FEA-020/031. The dry run uses the generator's fill function (adjacency, run count, share cap). If V_req exceeds the fill capacity at max_runs, return AVAILABILITY_DAYS and state the run day to add. C9, C5.
  5. parameters tid.hard_share_max.short (N-06) = 0.20, final. Remove PROVISIONAL. Label: "top of tested doses, 10–20% zone 3 (hearing-2 coach-acad, CREDIBLE); counting all of E3 is conservative; practice UNKNOWN (factdesk3 §1)". Range 0.15–0.25. Short-race polarized override 0.20, not 0.12. C8, C5.
  6. 03 §3 FEA-010, parameters pred.band_same_distance_pct = 4. For a measured same-distance anchor ≤ 8 weeks old, B_fast = 4%. Basis: retest CV 3.4% recreational (ALLEGED), 1–1.5% trained (CORROBORATED). C9.
  7. 04 §2 H2. The merged BUILD/SPECIFIC phase has Phase.type = SPECIFIC for DST-018 and the quality allowance. C5.
  8. 13 P1/P2, 14 TST-070. Re-run P1 under R1–R2. Move P2's MSP to week 9. Add case 2 (3-run 10K) and case 4 (H2, same-distance band) as fixtures. C11, C13.

Optional improvements

  • Cap running-minute growth per rung of the return ladder at 1.5× (R1 → R2 now doubles).
  • Relabel ctx.lowfreq_quality_max = 1: "1 quality + 1 long + 1 easy" is CORROBORATED as practice.

d2-roomB.md

D2 Room B — Half & marathon (sub-council round 2)

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

Systems Architect: All six round-1 changes are applied (02 §3/§5, 05 §4, DST-006/007, SES-M-003/005, INV-26, 14 §10b). Residue: race.longest_floor_ratio.M is declared only for daniels, so INV-26 is ambiguous. New bug: at the time cap the distance stop is 180 × S_slow, so the 10% planning margin becomes a real stop. P3 stops at 24.9 km after ~162 min, while MSG-DST-LR3H promises "about 30 km". Context Realist: Slow runners fare worse. Above ~7:41/km real easy pace, the 21.1 km floor needs > 180 min at S_slow → LONG_RUN_UNREACHABLE_PRESET. Galloway (26.4 km in 180 min) fails too, so run-walkers get no plan. First Principles: Both sides of N-01 are weak. The cap is convention: duration–injury UNKNOWN [h2 med-acad/crit]; 2.5–3 h is CORROBORATED practice [h2 coach-field]. The counter-signal is one cohort [fd3 §5, CORROBORATED, observational], with Fokkema pointing the same way [fd3 §5, ALLEGED]. So: fix the margin bug, make the floor reachable, and offer the cohort's distance as an opt-in. Executor: One rule, a few parameters, fixtures. Extension runs must never enter feasibility. Architect: Keep the share monotone: max(min(L,180)/0.50, L/0.60). The ceiling then becomes 240/0.60 against the ultra's 330/0.60. The 50K's AER 4 (450 min/week) blocks goal-shopping, except through the baseline hold after a family switch. Context Realist: N-07: the 4-run requirement is ungraded. FIRST and Galloway use 3 days [fd3 §6, CORROBORATED structure]. Toresdahl only reinforces this.

d2-roomC.md

Design room C, sub-council round 2: 50–100 km road (artifact v2)

Session (condensed, speaker-labelled)

First Principles: FEA-005 says that when the time cap binds, the goal is "never infeasible". But the ultra distance floor defaults to longest_ratio_max (100K: 40 km), and INV-25 blocks any plan below it. So a 330-min run at 8:45/km (37.7 km) fails at S8.

Contrarian: Then make that deliberate. I withdraw my dissent if two conditions hold: stretch ends at an explicit distance floor, and a cut-off that even the optimistic range cannot meet fails closed. Duration has no injury data [hearing-2 med, UNKNOWN].

Status-quo: X-1: 330 min fits a 100K; a 50K borrows it. 50K plans peak around 3:15 to 26 mi [factdesk3 §8, CORROBORATED range] and 32–42 km [hearing-2 coach-field, CORROBORATED]. A 4 h cap for the 50K follows that practice and halves the cliff.

Outsider: 13 §6 calls case 1 "feasible", but 02's AER 4 (450 min) refuses me on 6 h.

Contrarian: Keep AER 4. Plans peak at 65–88 km.

First Principles: Those are plans, not minimums. The 100K was lowered on the finish-tier argument (45–50 km/week for ~89 km [wg2 §4, CORROBORATED as description]). The same logic applies to the 50K: AER 3 plus TOL 4 gives 360 min. Vote 3–1; Contrarian dissents.

Outsider: The heat card offers "the 0.70 length" but never says that a peak is lost.

Case walk-throughs

  • C1: first 50K on 360 min/week (S_slow 6:45, L30 22 km, 18 weeks).
  • As written: AER4 = 450 min, so AVAILABILITY_TIME (+90 min). This contradicts the TST-070 fixture.
  • With RC4: V_req = max(300, 360, 337, 352) = 360, so the plan is feasible or LOW_SLACK, with zero spare minutes.
  • C2: 100K on 540 min/week, 18-day fever starting week 11.
  • Before the fever: L_req 257 min, V_req 450 min → feasible. The peak comes in weeks 8–9.
  • First week back: GEN-013 sets the long run to 154 min.
  • Second week back: L30_eff still holds the 257-min run, so a +67% jump passes the spike cap. This is a defect.
  • With RC5: the long run grows 154 → 205 min and falls short. The last peak is 77 days out, more than 70, so there is no credit. PHASES_DO_NOT_FIT → hold the plan and offer a later date. The 50K option fails too. Honest, but poorly explained (O5).
  • AER/TOL 5 → 4 check: 450 min ≈ 70 km/week, which is still above the finish and Bronze tiers [wg2 §4]. Back-to-backs still need TOL 5, and V_peak grows to 600 min when time allows. Safe.
  • C3: masters runner, 58, hot 100K (480 min/week, S_slow 7:40).
  • V_req = 307 / 0.60 = 511 min → infeasible, short by 31 min. The 50K is offered (with RC3: V_req 400 min) and is feasible.
  • Peaks come about every 3 weeks (deload every 3) and last more than 4 h, so NUT-034 shows every time.
  • Accepting the 0.70 length loses that peak with no notice, because INV-04 forbids making it up.
  • C3 variants:
  • 8:45/km: the long run is capped at 330 min (37.7 km). INV-25 blocks it as written. With RC1 (floor 33.3 km) → stretch.
  • 10:00/km: 33.0 km at the cap is below the floor → infeasible, 50K offered.
  • Cut-off: the low end of ultra_range is ~12:30 h, and the race cut-off is 12 h → RC2 → infeasible.
  • X-1 shopping: a runner at 8:00/km gets a 180-min long run for the marathon but 250 min for a 50K. RC3 lowers that to 240 min, a gain of at most 60 min, and only with TOL 4, DUR 4 and V ≥ L / 0.60.

Required changes

  1. 03 FEA-005, 07 INV-25, parameters race.longest_floor_ratio: explicit ultra distance floors.
  • Floors: 50K 2.00 (25 km), 100K 3.00 (33.3 km).
  • Stretch applies only while the time-capped run at S_slow still reaches the floor. Otherwise LONG_RUN_UNREACHABLE_PRESET (infeasible).
  • INV-25 checks ultras against the capped L_req_time and this floor.
  • Why: removes a contradiction and settles the dissent.
  • Design defaults below practice (30–42 km) [wg2 §4, ALLEGED; hearing-2 coach-field, CORROBORATED].
  • Criteria: C5, C9, C7.
  1. 03, new FEA-006: cut-off check.
  • cutoff_h < ultra_range low → CUTOFF_UNREACHABLE (infeasible).
  • cutoff_h < ultra_range high → CUTOFF_RISK (stretch).
  • Applies whether or not the time cap binds (round-1 O2 was only half applied).
  • Criteria: C9, C12.
  1. Parameters lr.ultra_time_cap_min, 09 DST-008, 05 LOD-020: cap per distance.
  • 50K: 240 min (range 210–270). 100K: 330 min.
  • Evidence: factdesk3 §8; hearing-2 coach-field.
  • Guard: a long run over 180 min also needs TOL L4 with confidence measured.
  • Criteria: C5, C7, C8.
  1. 02 §5: cmp.required.50K.AER 4 → 3.
  • The ladder becomes M 300/240 → 50K 300/360 → 100K 450/360.
  • Evidence: wg2 §4 tier; the factdesk3 §8 plans describe practice, not minimums.
  • Contrarian dissent recorded.
  • Criteria: C9, C1.
  1. 07 GEN-013, 05 LOD-011: reset L30_eff after a gap.
  • After a G1/G2 gap, runs from before the gap leave the L30_eff window.
  • Evidence: spike risk [factdesk3 §7, CORROBORATED]. Post-gap handling is UNKNOWN [factdesk2 §1], so this is the conservative default, consistent with GEN-012.
  • Criteria: C6, C7.
  1. 11 NUT-034: make the cost of the shorter run visible.
  • "Start earlier" comes first.
  • If the runner accepts the 0.70 length, log it as a runner shortening and say "your build moves back about 2 weeks".
  • Re-run FEA via 08. INV-04 is unchanged.
  • Criteria: C6, C7, C12.
  1. 13 §6, 14 TST-070: fixtures.
  • Correct C1.
  • Add fixtures for the 8:45 and 10:00 variants, the cut-off case, the C2 illness case and an accepted NUT-034 shortening.
  • Criteria: C11, C13.

Optional improvements

  • O1. NUT-034 card: "carry fluid or plan water stops", since drinking to thirst needs access [hearing-2 nutr-acad, CORROBORATED]. Design default.
  • O2. Beyond ~2 h, prefer an electrolyte drink over plain water, labelled "not EAH prevention". [hearing-2 nutr-field: direction CORROBORATED; acad: contested.]
  • O3. Heat exposure starts with short easy runs, never the long run [hearing-2 nutr-field, CORROBORATED direction].
  • O4. C1's peak equals the TOL 4 threshold, so deloads keep V28 below it; count demand as met at the peak week.
  • O5. When a re-plan fails, show the earliest feasible date and the reason (peak no longer credited).

Criteria check for this focus

  • R2 is undone by INV-25 (fixed by RC1).
  • The R7 fixture for C1 is wrong (fixed by RC7).

  1. Road 50K plans: longest run in time, and peak weekly time, for runners at 7:00/km or slower.
  2. Frandsen 2025: does the 30-day reference include runs before an illness gap?
  3. Recreational 100K/50K finish time against marathon PB, to check pred.ultra_exp_*.

p1-roomA.md

Plenary 1 — Room A: architecture & rule engine (v3)

Members: Contrarian, First Principles (FP), Executor, Status-quo (SQ), Context Realist (CR).

Session (condensed)

Executor: The 05 long-run formula uses max(lr_cap, L_hold). INV-13 checks LR ≤ preset cap with no hold exception. So REP-001 clamps the run, INV-27 then fails, and the plan fails closed. Contrarian: A goal change triggers this. ADP-080 regenerates from the current state, the only family-switch guard is the ultra→road cap of 240, and "this season" (FEA-025) is undefined after a switch. CR: FEA-023 says HAB is not a gate in H1, but FEA-031 still turns HAB runs into AVAILABILITY_DAYS. At 2 runs a week, the 0.45 share cap cannot be met under "easy ≤ LR". FP: The 10% S_slow margin turns a distance the runner has already run into a time shortfall. Exposure is distance-only [factdesk4 §3, CORROBORATED]; logged pace is only moderately reliable [factdesk2 §6, CREDIBLE]. Executor: H4 reads "extended BASE, or two macrocycles", step 8 splits every plan over 24 weeks, and how to split into several seasons is not defined. Contrarian: On P-04, routing the slowest runners to the marathon keeps X-1 closed, since both ceilings are 240. Keep it. On P-05, a blocking verdict rests on an UNKNOWN exponent. SQ/CR: Riegel's 1.06 is the only grounded value [factdesk4 §10, CORROBORATED; Comrades 1.05 ALLEGED].

Case walk-throughs

  1. Lea, switching from marathon to 10K. She is in week 9 of 18: 4 runs, V28 330, L30_eff 160 min measured, 10K 50:00 measured 40 days ago. She switches to a 10K in 6 weeks, target 48:30.
  • Expected: an H2 plan with long runs ≤ ~130 min; stretch (fast band, edge 47:02).
  • v3: L_target = max(60, L_hold 160) gives LR 160. INV-13 fails (cap 0.40 × 330 = 132), REP-001 clamps, INV-27 fails, and the plan fails closed. With INV-13 fixed alone she would get 160-min long runs in a 10K plan.
  1. Tom, 5K → 100K over 100 weeks (H5). Runs 3 days a week (5 available), 540 min.
  • The dry run reaches V_req 500 and L_req 300 in about 56 weeks, so the plan is feasible.
  • Breaks:
  • A multi-season split is undefined: 24/24/24/28 exceeds season_max.
  • A season that ends in a test still gets a TRANSITION (≤ 50% volume, no long run). L30_eff then decays and the long run must regrow each season.
  • The dry run ignores these season boundaries, so the verdict is optimistic.
  1. Raj, half marathon in 4 weeks (H1). L30 17.0 km / 100 min, measured.
  • S_slow 6:46 gives a 16.2 km demand of 109.6 min.
  • 100 < 109.6 → CURRENT_STATE_SHORT → infeasible, and a 10K is offered, even though 17 km ≥ 16.2 km.
  1. Mia, 2 days a week (Tue 40 / Sat 55).
  • 10K finish → AVAILABILITY_DAYS. The 5K option fails the same way and move_date is skipped, so only a base plan is offered.
  • A 5K in 3 weeks passes FEA-023 but fails FEA-031: the two rules contradict each other.
  • Her base plan has no family, and its 0.45 share cap is unsatisfiable, so it fails validation.
  1. P-04, 10:00/km runner aiming for a 50K. 24 km < the 25 km floor → the marathon is offered. Consistent with X-1. Keep.
  2. P-05, 100K with a 12:15 cut-off, T_M 4:50. The low edge is 12:29 with exponent 1.10 (UNREACHABLE) but 12:04 with 1.06 (AT_RISK). The verdict flips on an UNKNOWN parameter.

Required changes

  1. 07 §5 INV-13, §1. INV-13 becomes LR ≤ max(preset cap, L_hold). §1 should state that a T5 floor beats T5 caps but never a T0–T3 limit. Case 1. C5, C11.
  2. 08 ADP-080, 05 LOD-005, 03 FEA-025: carry-over on goal change.
  • On a change of distance, L_hold = min(L30_eff, max(L_target_new, lr_cap(new preset, V_hold))). This replaces the ultra→road special case.
  • FEA-025a/b apply only when the distance is unchanged. Otherwise a new season starts with full phase minimums, and the deload counter carries over.
  • Case 1. C6, C5, C3.
  1. 03 §2, FEA-023, FEA-025b, INV-25. When L30_eff_dist is measured, the long-run demand is met if it is ≥ race / longest_ratio_max. S_slow then only converts future planning time. Case 3. C9, C5.
  2. 03 FEA-031. In H1, runs needed = min(HAB runs, sustained F28), consistent with FEA-023. Case 4. C5, C2.
  3. 05 LOD-020, parameters lr.pct_cap.short, 03 §1.
  • Add a 2-run value ≥ 0.50 (default 0.60, design default).
  • Define a family base (short caps) for goals none, maintain and base.
  • Case 4. C5, C11.
  1. 04 §2–3, FEA-020: deterministic horizons.
  • Split into seasons only when W > 52. H4 is one macrocycle with extended BASE and FEA-038 checkpoints.
  • Use n = ⌈W/season_max⌉ seasons of near-equal length, with the longer ones last.
  • After a test, schedule a deload week; TRANSITION only after races.
  • The dry run models season boundaries.
  • Case 2. C3, C5, C9.
  1. 03 §2, FEA-003/005: one long-run formula for every family.
  • L_req_time = max(min(L_ratio, cap), floor/S, dur_time), admissible iff floor/S ≤ lr.floor_time_max_min[distance]. For ultras this equals the cap (50K 240, 100K 330), which reproduces v3, so FEA-005 is deleted.
  • LONG_RUN_TIME_CAPPED is a stretch reason for time goals and information for finish goals, in every family (Contrarian dissent: keep ultras at stretch).
  • P-04 kept; the message shows the runner's own numbers.
  • C5, C9, C12.
  1. parameters pred.ultra_exp_low = 1.06, FEA-006 (P-05). A block uses the grounded bound; 1.30 stays as the high edge (UNKNOWN). C9, C8.
  2. 13 §2, 14 §10c.
  • Fixtures TST-079…082 for cases 1–4.
  • Property test: every feasible grid persona validates with zero repairs (generator–validator parity).
  • C11, C13.

Optional improvements

  • Goal.milestones[]: runner-chosen races that end non-final seasons, each checked by FEA.

Criteria check for this focus

  • C3 Partly: H4 ambiguity, multi-season split, transitions.
  • C4 Met: once change 4 is applied.
  • C5 Partly: INV-13 vs INV-27, FEA-023 vs FEA-031, the H4 "or".
  • C6 Partly: goal-change carry-over.
  • C9 Partly: S_slow margin, seasons missing from the dry run, P-05.
  • C11 Partly: no cross-family fixtures; family undefined for none.
  • C13 Partly: no parity property.
  • C1, C2, C7 Met.

Information requests

  1. Recreational 50K/100K finish-time exponents relative to the marathon (P-05).
  2. 5K/10K programmes or cohorts at 2 runs a week: completion and injury.
  3. Frandsen 2025: does the reference window hold for runs over 2 h?

Expert requests

  1. Coaching (field): long-run length after a marathon→10K switch, and at season boundaries in multi-year ultra development.
  2. Knowledge modelling (academic): validator independence when it shares demand formulas with the generator.
